#2971. Sixers-Cavs by the numbers: Pressure, threes and a 29-point rematch

Sixers-Cavs by the numbers: Pressure, threes and a 29-point rematch originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

The Sixers and Cavaliers have already played one game that looked like two.

Cleveland led by 29 points during the Sixers' home opener on Oct. 22 and carried a 69-45 advantage into halftime. Philadelphia responded by winning the second half, 69-37, and completing a 114-106 comeback.

It was the Sixers' largest comeback since the NBA began maintaining detailed play-by-play data in 1997-98. Only nine other teams during that span had overcome a deficit of at least 29 points.

Fifteen days later, the teams will meet again Friday night at Rocket Arena. This time, the matchup counts toward the NBA Cup standings.

The 8-1 Sixers have won five consecutive games and opened group play with a 98-93 victory over the Knicks. The 4-4 Cavs have dropped two straight and will make their Cup debut.

Here are five numbers that help frame the rematch.

Philadelphia's comeback required more than a hot shooting stretch.

The Sixers committed 15 turnovers in the first half, allowing Cleveland to build a 22-6 advantage in points off turnovers. Their pressure then changed the game after halftime.

The Cavs committed 21 turnovers by the end of the night. What had been a 16-point Cleveland advantage in points off turnovers finished even at 22 apiece.

"They upped their pressure and physicality," Cavs head coach Kenny Atkinson said after the game. "It was pretty clear. We did not handle it well."

The Sixers scored 64 of their 69 second-half points through their starting lineup. Tyrese Maxey finished with 28 points, and Philadelphia turned a game that appeared finished into a franchise-record comeback.

The first half of the opening meeting now looks like an exception.

The Sixers have committed 12.9 turnovers per 100 possessions this season, the NBA's second-lowest rate. They are also third in turnover differential, averaging 3.4 fewer giveaways than their opponents.

That is notable for a team that entered the season intending to play faster. Philadelphia has pushed the ball without routinely surrendering possessions.

LeBron James' presence as the primary facilitator has helped. So has Maxey's willingness to move the ball when defenses trap him. Joel Embiid has averaged 5.2 assists, third among qualified centers, and the Sixers have increasingly trusted VJ Edgecombe to make decisions late in games.

Cleveland's defense has not forced many mistakes. The Cavs enter Friday near the bottom of the league in opponent turnover rate.

The first meeting swung when the Sixers stopped giving away possessions and forced Cleveland into mistakes of its own.

The Sixers have allowed 105.1 points per 100 possessions, the NBA's second-best defensive rating. Cleveland enters with the league's ninth-ranked offense.

The Cavs are the only top-10 offense the Sixers have faced through their first nine games.

Philadelphia has held opponents to a 50 percent effective field-goal percentage and 31.8 percent shooting from three-point range. Its defensive shot profile has been encouraging, too. Only 46 percent of opposing three-point attempts have been classified as wide open, the fifth-lowest share in the league, and opponents have taken just 22 percent of their threes from the corners.

There is reason for caution. Based on the quality of shots Philadelphia has allowed, only Washington's opponents have underperformed their expected effective field-goal percentage by a wider margin. The Sixers have defended well, but opponents are unlikely to remain this cold indefinitely.

Cleveland, with Donovan Mitchell, James Harden and Evan Mobley, can test whether those numbers hold against high-level individual shot creation.

The Cavs have attempted exactly 42 three-pointers per game and made 37.2 percent of them.

Cleveland went 7 for 29 from beyond the arc in Philadelphia. Mitchell, Harden and De'Andre Hunter combined to shoot 3 for 17. The Cavs scored 60 points in the paint and still reached 106, but their 24.1 percent performance from three prevented them from stopping the Sixers' comeback.

Friday's game could look considerably different if Cleveland generates its normal volume.

The Sixers have controlled the three-point line well so far, but Mitchell is coming off a 27-point performance against Oklahoma City and scored 32 in the first meeting with Philadelphia. Harden added 16 points that night, although his six turnovers became costly as the Sixers increased their pressure.

Philadelphia has been outrebounded by 5.1 per game. The Cavs rank sixth with 47.5 rebounds per contest.

The Sixers have already shown they can survive a major deficit on the glass. They beat Milwaukee by 11 despite getting outrebounded by 22. That is not a formula they will want to test regularly against Cleveland's size.

Mobley had 18 points and eight rebounds in the first meeting. Jarrett Allen added 14 points and seven boards, helping Cleveland win the rebounding battle, 46-44.

Whether Allen plays Friday remains uncertain.

Cleveland listed Allen as questionable because of a right knee injury after he left Wednesday's loss to Oklahoma City in the first quarter. Allen collided knee-to-knee with Thunder forward Chet Holmgren and did not return.

The Cavs also listed Peyton Watson as questionable with right hamstring soreness and Tyrese Proctor as questionable with an ankle injury. Proctor left Wednesday's game during the fourth quarter. Jaylon Tyson is out with a right ankle sprain.

Allen's availability could significantly alter the matchup on the glass. If he sits, Thomas Bryant would likely handle more of the center minutes behind Mobley. If he plays, Cleveland will have the same starting frontcourt that outrebounded the Sixers during the first meeting.

Atkinson has emphasized the possession battle during Cleveland's two-game slide.

"You've got to get more shots than the other team," he said this week. "Rebounding is a big part of it."

That also carries added importance in the NBA Cup, where point differential is the second tiebreaker after head-to-head results.

Philadelphia enters Friday at 1-0 with a plus-five differential in East Group B. Miami is also 1-0 at plus-20, while Cleveland is playing its first group game.

The Sixers already know they can erase 29 points against the Cavs. A cleaner start would be far more useful than proving it twice.

#2970. Is Cam Skattebo playing this week? Latest injury update on Giants RB

The New York Giants are dealing with an injury to their clear-cut top running back coming off their bye week.

Cam Skattebo is dealing with a hamstring injury that he suffered on the final carry of his Week 7 game against the Houston Texans. He pulled up lame at the end of a 42-yard run in overtime and ceded the field for the final snap, which resulted in a game-winning, 30-yard Devin Singletary touchdown run.

Skattebo didn't seem concerned about his injury after the game, so many believed the second-year running back would be good to go for the team's Week 9 game against the Philadelphia Eagles.

Instead, Skattebo has continued to be absent from practice, which has thrown his status into flux.

Here's what to know about Skattebo's injury and when he might return to the field for the division-leading Giants.

The Giants have not yet provided a definitive timeline for Skattebo's return to the field.

Harbaugh didn't seem overly concerned about Skattebo's absence being of the long-term variety. He referred to the running back as "day-to-day" entering the practice week while saying the team would take a wait-and-see approach with the 24-year-old.

During a Wednesday, Nov. 4 press conference, Harbaugh also alluded to the tricky nature of predicting recovery for soft-tissue injuries, like hamstring problems.

"Skatt's got a hamstring, so you know how that goes," Harbaugh said, referring to Skattebo by his nickname. "We'll see."

Devin Singletary is currently entrenched as the clear-cut backup to Skattebo. The 29-year-old veteran is in his third season with the Giants but has played sparingly to date, racking up 80 yards and a touchdown on 18 carries.

Singletary produced exactly 437 rushing yards in each of his first two seasons with the Giants while serving largely as a backup or third-string option. The 2019 third-round pick posted three seasons with at least 1,000 scrimmage yards from 2021-23 and would get a lion's share of New York's backfield workload if Skattebo can't play.

The Giants presently have three running backs on their 53-man roster, as normal backup Tyrone Tracy Jr. is on IR with a hamstring injury while veteran Najee Harris was released earlier in the season. Below is a look at the pecking order within the unit.

Desrosiers is an undrafted rookie who played collegiately at Louisville, UMass and Memphis before joining the NFL ranks. He originally signed with the Los Angeles Chargers but was released following final cuts and joined their practice squad.

The Giants inked Desrosier to their 53-man roster to poach him from the Chargers, so he may get a first crack at backing up Singletary with Skattebo out.

The Giants also have Grant Finley, Eric Gray and Phil Mafah on the practice squad, so any could be called up to provide additional depth. Gray has almost exclusively been with the Giants since being selected in the fifth round of the 2023 NFL Draft, save for a month-long stint with the Cleveland Browns' practice squad earlier this season. His familiarity with the Giants and special teams skills could prove to be a tiebreaker when New York assesses its potential call-ups.

#2969. Mississippi Senate: Longshot Ty Pinkins says he aimed to spoil Hyde-Smith, boost Colom vote

Longshot Mississippi Senate candidate Ty Pinkins said that at first wanted to win — but that he stayed in to spoil.

Pinkins told a local TV station that after an April poll showed he had no chance of winning he remained in the race in order to boost Democratic nominee Scott Colom's chances of beating Republican incumbent Sen. Cindy Hyde-Smith.

Pinkins' purported strategy might pay off.

Colom is narrowly leading Hyde-Smith in ongoing counting by just 1,936 votes, a margin of just 0.2 percentage points, with 96.2% of the vote counted, according to the Associated Press. That margin is about one-tenth the size of Pinkins' share of the vote, 2.1 percentage points.

Pinkins has won just 20,010 votes so far out of the nearly 952,000 votes already counted.

"The only thing I did was stay in the race and let people shoot arrows at me," Pinkins said in an interview with WTVA in Mississippi on Thursday. "That is it. And that's a ... low price to pay to get someone in this race that I think can help people in the Delta and all across this state."

"The portion of the vote that I drew — and people were concerned about splitting the Black vote — what I actually did was split the Republican vote," he said.

Pinkins told the station in Tupelo that he intentionally avoided campaigning in areas with large Black populations to avoid cutting into the vote share of Colom, who, like Pinkins, is Black.

That tactic worked, he said, as the election results show he got most of his support from areas with relatively low Black populations.

"I'll be the boogeyman," he said. "But I'll tell you this: The independent in this race pulled most of his votes from a Republican, and for the first time since Reconstruction, a Democrat who is a Black man is leading."

Pinkins, a lawyer who previously ran for Mississippi secretary of state, was the Democratic nominee for Senate in 2024. He was crushed by Republican Sen. Roger Wicker, who won nearly 63% of the vote, compared with about 37% for Pinkins.

Despite that showing, the U.S. Army veteran decided to take another crack at the Senate in 2026.

But in the spring, he saw the writing on the wall for his candidacy.

"I got in this race to win," Pinkins told WTVA. "In April, I knew I was going to lose, but I also knew that I was pulling 70% of the Republican vote based on the [SPLC Action Fund and Impact Research] poll."

SPLC Action Fund, when it released the poll, said, "Sagging favorability numbers for Sen. Cindy Hyde-Smith could open up a path to victory for Democrats."

"Independent Ty Pinkins has the potential to play spoiler for either candidate," SPLC Action Fund said in April.

In the WTVA interview, Pinkins said, "I probably attended maybe, maybe six events over the summer" as part of his campaign.

"Six events. I don't have to," Pinkins said. "Why? I'm not going to win. So why go to these Black communities that he's going to?"

Pinkins said all he had to do was visit counties that have relatively low Black populations and "talk to some Republicans that said they're going to vote for me."

"They were literally calling me and sending me emails: 'We're going to vote for you because we don't want to waste our vote on Cindy Hyde-Smith, we don't want to hold our nose and vote for her, so we're going to vote for you. Please stay in the race,'" he said.

The campaigns of Colom and Hyde-Smith did not immediately respond to CNBC's requests for comment.

#2968. Data center double-down: Hassett says blue wave won't stop AI build-out

National Economic Council Director Kevin Hassett said Friday he expects data center growth to continue as projected, despite the blue-wave election that saw voters overwhelmingly favor limiting the tech infrastructure buildout.

Hassett, on CNBC's "Squawk on the Street," acknowledged the dozens of measures on states' midterm ballots seeking to constrain data centers, a crucial part of President Donald Trump's push to win the global artificial intelligence race.

In at least 46 of the roughly 50 such initiatives on ballots in 2026, majorities voted to restrict development of the facilities, according to Ballotpedia's tally.

"We respect the localities that decide that's what they want to do," Hassett said. "But America is a big place, and right now, the data centers scheduled over the next couple of years aren't affected by these ballot initiatives."

"And the stuff after that, you know, there's plenty of room in the U.S. for data center growth to continue, as it was projected ahead of the election," he said.

Hassett said he expects data centers will continue to expand in states with more welcoming governors, and he suggested that even hostile states will eventually come around.

"That's exactly what's going to happen," he said. "That, or what'll happen will be that the benefits of the data centers will be so obvious that people decide to change their minds about the way they regulate them."

Hassett also blamed Thursday's tech-driven market plunge on "uncertainty about what the political change is going to mean for data centers going forward."

"We've now got a ... new majority that maybe is going to go in there and muddle around in there, and I think that markets are anxious about that," he said.

"But I think that the economic strength that we see everywhere else is so strong, and of course, in the end, you could take something that's growing as fast as the data centers and make it grow a little bit slower, and you still have a blockbuster economy."

"And so, I think we're still very, very optimistic about what's going to happen going forward," he said.

The CNBC Voter Poll found 56% of midterm voters identified data centers as either a crucial or major issue — and of those who considered it crucial, 62% favored the generic Democratic candidate.

Trump has strongly embraced AI and data centers and has even tried to reverse their seeming unpopularity by rebranding the tech as "super intelligence," or SI.

In a Truth Social post Friday, Trump made clear that despite the midterm losses, his support for the nascent industry has not faltered.

"As President, I am 100% in favor of Super Intelligence (SI), and will work with the many States in the U.S. that want to become RICH by having them," Trump wrote. "They are GOLD, and only foolish leadership will say 'NO' to the riches they bring!"

#2966. Microsoft plans to use fewer neoclouds as AI buildout shows signs of strain

Microsoft is shrinking its roster of neocloud partners as it focuses on work with a few key suppliers and builds out its own data centers, according to people familiar with the matter.

The software giant's plans pose another potential challenge for a corner of the artificial intelligence market that's showing signs of strain due to rising borrowing costs, hefty debt loads and concerns about whether the buildout will slow down.

Microsoft has forged relationships in recent years with a number of neoclouds, including CoreWeave, Nebius, Chronoscale, IREN, Lambda and Nscale, as well as with Oracle, which is pouring money into building out AI infrastructure.

Microsoft is now in the evaluation stage and hasn't decided which neoclouds it will stop using, said two sources, who asked not to be named because the talks are confidential. One of the people said Microsoft wants to prioritize its own data center footprint. Bloomberg reported on the plans earlier. Two people said that Microsoft opted not to renew a deal with Oracle.

A Microsoft spokesperson said in an emailed statement that its infrastructure strategy is "built on flexibility and choice."

"We combine Microsoft-owned datacenters, leased facilities and strategic partnerships with third-party providers to deliver the capacity our customers need, where and when they need it," the spokesperson said. "We regularly review these investments against customer demand. That includes tracking delivery and adjusting plans as circumstances change."

The neoclouds have been a turbulent trade on Wall Street in recent weeks as rising interest rates added to fears that many companies have become overly reliant on debt in rushing to build capacity. Shares of CoreWeave, Nebius and IREN are all down by roughly half over the past month even after rallying on Friday.

A recent industry review counted 323 neoclouds, and experts have predicted that many of them won't survive.

In its earnings report on Thursday, IREN said it handed over data center capacity to Microsoft in August, but Microsoft requested a networking equipment upgrade that will result in the infrastructure coming online in January or February instead of this year.

Microsoft finance chief Amy Hood said on the software company's fiscal first-quarter earnings call in October that some cloud contracts involving graphics processing units were delayed until the first half of 2027, affecting Azure cloud growth. Microsoft reported $45.2 billion worth of capital expenditures and finance leases during the latest quarter, and said capex spending will accelerate in the second half of the fiscal year.

CoreWeave and Nebius are both scheduled to report quarterly results next week. IREN shares popped 16% on Friday after the company said AI revenue more than doubled from the prior quarter to $200 million. IREN co-CEO Daniel Roberts told CNBC that there's a "disconnect between what we're seeing in public market and what we're seeing at the customer level."

However, he acknowledged the challenge that market conditions present.

"Capital markets have become more selective in the last few weeks, and the questions have undoubtedly moved from how big the contract is to whether it can be delivered, financed, and run at a profit," Roberts said on the earnings call.

Microsoft has already slashed its work with U.K.-based neocloud Nscale, walking away from a site that it had intended to use in England. Darren Hardman, Microsoft's CEO for the U.K and Ireland, told CNBC in October that the decision had to do with Nscale's ability to get access to electricity.

The Financial Times reported this week that Microsoft decided against renewing a contract with Nscale in Texas. Nscale filed to go public in mid-September but hasn't updated its prospectus since then. The company said in its IPO filing that it expects Microsoft to be a "significant customer in future periods."

Microsoft's big turn to neoclouds came after the launch of ChatGPT in 2022, which led to a surge in demand for GPUs. In looking elsewhere, Microsoft signed up CoreWeave, which was a privately held startup at the time with roots in cryptocurrency mining. When CoreWeave went public last year, it said 62% of its 2024 revenue came from Microsoft.

At the time of ChatGPT's release, OpenAI only used Azure. Now the company's cloud suppliers also include Amazon, Cerebras, CoreWeave, Google and Oracle.

Microsoft's spokesperson said that its approach to reviewing neocloud investments is "a normal and responsible way to manage large, complex infrastructure projects," and that "the pace of our new agreements reflects what we need today."

—CNBC's Kif Leswing contributed to this report.

#2965. Coinbase net income surges 66% in third quarter, benefiting from bitcoin investment gains

Coinbase Global earned third-quarter adjusted net income of $719 million, up 66% from $433 million a year ago, largely due to a recovery in the cryptocurrency market.

Coinbase said it earned $2.51 a share in the latest quarter, although it wasn't immediately clear if that compared with the 6-cent-per-share profit that Wall Street analysts had estimated. Revenue of $1.20 billion trailed the $1.233 billion expected by analysts polled by LSEG.

Net income at the San Francisco-based platform surged thanks to the value of its volatile cryptocurrency investment portfolio, which can swing wildly even when the composition of the underlying holdings are little changed.

The third quarter value of Coinbase's crypto investment portfolio climbed due to a resurgence in the digital asset market. The price of bitcoin — often used as a gauge for all crypto assets — rose 39% in the three-month period ended Sept. 30 and the flagship cryptocurrency recently changed hands at roughly $80,809.

In the third quarter, Coinbase deepened its push into non-crypto products and services, including artificial intelligence integrations for traders, tokenized real-world assets and prediction markets.

In July, VP of product Max Branzburg told CNBC that Coinbase would debut an "everything exchange" spotlighting tokenized stocks and other real-world assets, derivatives trading, prediction markets and early-stage token sales. The trading platform also introduced Coinbase for Agents in early October, supporting users that want to use ChatGPT, Muse or other AI agents to manage their portfolios or research and trade various assets.

Coinbase also expanded its prediction market offerings, making a wider range of event contracts available to users.

In the third quarter, prediction markets revenue grew to more than $200 million on an annual basis, while the number of traders on those markets increased 60% compared with the second quarter.

#2964. Brandon Aubrey has confidence in everything around him

When it comes to kickers, the Dallas Cowboys are quite fortunate. This season has been a little bit rough in some moments, but ultimately the Cowboys still employ one of the best around in Brandon Aubrey. It is unbelievable to consider that 2026 is already year number four for Aubrey.

Aubrey has been around the Cowboys so long that he has the proper marks to prove it. He was with the team when they last made the playoffs, a group that is starting to dwindle, and he even has a second contract with the club which means he has really walked through fire.

Aubrey is someone who takes care of himself and recently he has begun to do so by partnering with Invisalign and their "SHIFT Happens" mindset, something that he is considering both on and off the field. This week I had a chance to talk to him thanks to Invisalign, and you can watch our entire conversation below.

It was a treat to talk to Aubrey about some of the specific natures of kicking in today's NFL. We spent a good bit of time talking about his preparation and also how he has handled the new kickoff in terms of trying to pin opponents in specific circumstances.

One thing I was curious about was whether or not he believes his own success has played a part in how the Cowboys operate things on offense. My point was that some believe that Aubrey's range from downtown leads to Brian Schottenheimer becoming dependent on that when the Cowboys do get into field goal range. Obviously right now there is a bit of a feeling from fans that Schottenheimer is operating conservatively so take all of this with a grain of salt, but I found Brandon's answer on that specific question to be very interesting.

"I don't think when you're looking at, I don't I can't speak for play callers or coaching staff's mindsets, but for me if I were in that position I think I would have a couple of different ideas based on where I am on the field. And field goal range wherever that is for your kicker is one mindset, one play style especially if it's like a one score game."

"I'll say we've been in a lot of high scoring games so you can't necessarily think that way I guess. You'd always wanna score a touchdown if you can. But yeah, I think when you're in field goal range, you tend to not want to turn the ball over just because at that point you're very obviously giving up points. So yeah I can see possibly being a little more conservative when you're in field goal range. I don't know what our play calling mindset is. I'm not involved in any of those decisions. I just try to be ready to kick it when when my name's called."

This was a fair answer from Aubrey. He was specific in saying that he was only speaking for himself in a hypothetical world where he made those types of decisions, and he noted that you want to protect your opportunity to score points. That is fair and rational.

But the feel and reality of our current moment is hard to ignore. Consider that the Cowboys entered Week 9 with the lowest amount of attempted fourth down conversions (on 4th and 5 or shorter) when already inside of field goal range.

Again, this information really kind of tells you what you want it to. On one hand it suggests that the Cowboys try to secure points when it feels like they have them. On the other it opens the door of conversation as to whether or not they are perhaps too reliant upon that, which is why I asked Aubrey the question in the first place.

What do you think? Is the way that Dallas is going about things the right way, or should they adjust things in some way?

Our thanks to Brandon Aubrey and to Invisalign for the time.

Garcia's approach may conflict with what some advisors are telling Democrats — to go after corporate interests rather than Trump and his family.

Garcia, currently the committee's top Democrat, formally launched his bid to become chairman Thursday. He is widely expected to secure the position.

The White House has dismissed Democrats' planned investigations as politically motivated, setting up a potentially contentious showdown between the Trump administration and the incoming Democratic congressional majority.

The White House did not immediately respond to CNBC's request for comment.

#2962. FSU vs. Boston College: Score predictions, analysis from FSU Wire

It is time for the start of the Rick Stockstill era with the FSU Seminoles. Florida State is set to play the Boston College Eagles on Saturday on the road, their first game since firing Mike Norvell.

Stockstill has been on FSU's coaching staff since 2024, when he returned to Tallahassee as the program director of scouting for offense after 18 years as Middle Tennessee State's head coach. He played quarterback at FSU from 1977-81, finishing his career with 250 completions, 2,834 passing yards, and 26 passing touchdowns.

With FSU moving on from Norvell, he provides some veteran leadership for a program that he loves and he is excited to lead.

"I'm humbled, I'm honored to be here and I'm gonna bust my tail," he said in his introductory press conference. "I'm gonna bust my tail to give this program everything I have and everything we have. To make Coach Bowden and to make all the former players, the administration, our leadership, to make them proud of Florida State football."

Boston College (4-4, 2-3 ACC) will be a tough test for FSU (3-5, 1-4), which is looking to snap a three-game losing streak. The Seminoles are the more talented team and do have several advantages that should allow them to win the game, snapping their 12-game road losing streak.

While the Seminoles lead the all-time series 16-6, Boston College won the last meeting 28-13 back in 2024. FSU has won the past three games in Alumni Stadium and is 9-3 all time on the road.

Here are our predictions for FSU's game against Boston College.

It just feels like it's FSU's time to finally get a win on the road. Boston College is up and down this season and presents the best possible chance to get a win. The Eagles won't wow you on either side of the ball, and it'll be Rick Stockstill starting 1-0.

This game will be pretty tight throughout, but I feel Florida State will be able to take advantage of the terrible red zone defense of Boston College and be able to pull away in the second half.

Florida State has enough offensive firepower to keep this close, but the Seminoles' turnover issues are difficult to ignore. Ashton Daniels has thrown 11 interceptions, including three against Clemson, while BC's run-heavy approach is well suited to attack an FSU defense that has struggled to get off the field consistently. FSU has also lost three straight entering the game and is navigating a coaching transition, while Boston College is 4-4 and playing at home. I expect a competitive game, but I trust the Eagles slightly more to avoid the costly mistakes that have repeatedly hurt Florida State.

FSU's first game under interim head coach Rick Stockstill will be on the road, but the Seminoles have a great shot to snap their road losing streak. I'm expecting the Seminoles to come playing harder and more motivated, picking up a win over a struggling Boston College team.

#2961. Bucs at Bears Q&A: Bears look to continue their dominance

The Chicago Bears haven't trailed in a game since October 11th. They haven't trailed with Caleb Williams since September 20th. They look to continue their domination against the Tampa Bay Buccaneers on Sunday Night Football.

The Bucs have won 3 of their last 4 and look to continue their winning ways against the Bears. To get you set for this game, we chatted with Ira Kaufman, a long-time Bucs reporter (and Hall of Fame voter), currently with JoeBucsFan, for insight on the Bears. Here's that Q&A.

1. Do the Tampa fans have any confidence in Todd Bowles? I know the Bucs are just a half game out of first, but this is a team that is 5-12 over their last 17 games.

Todd Bowles remains an unpopular figure for much of Buc Nation. Fans aren't happy with his clock management or his placid demeanor on the sidelines, suggesting he is being out-coached on a regular basis. A recent poll on the joebucsfan.com website revealed that more than 70 percent of voters have little or no confidence in Bowles, who is 38-38 since replacing Bruce Arians in 2022.

2. I feel like if Tampa is going to be competitive this week, Baker Mayfield is going to have to be effective in the passing game. Is he at a point where he can do that with the injured thumb? He's only thrown 14 passes the last two weeks.

Mayfield said this week that his injured right thumb has progressed to the point he no longer needs to wear a glove for support. Since he returned, Mayfield has thrown sparingly as Tampa Bay relied on a powerful ground game to defeat Carolina and Atlanta, climbing back into the race for the NFC South title.

3. The Bucs have certainly been powered by their running game, and the Bears have been susceptible to giving up some yards on the ground this season. Enter Jeffery Simmons. How do you think the Bucs can run the football against the Bears' new front with Big Jeff?

We just saw Simmons make an immediate impact as the Bears shut down Seattle on the road Monday night. However, the Seahawks don't have an elite running back like Bucky Irving, who is averaging 5.0 yards per carry. Add in Kenneth Gainwell and a physical offensive line, and it's fair to say Chicago's rush defense faces a major challenge. It's the best way for the Bucs to keep Caleb Williams and company watching from the sidelines.

4. Who is a lesser-known player on the Bucs offense and one on the Bucs defense that you think could have an impact in this game that fans may not know a lot about?

The Bucs are looking for a No. 1 target to emerge in the wake of Mike Evans' departure to the 49ers. Rookie Ted Hurst has shown flashes as a big-bodied WR who can get downfield. He earned Mayfield's trust during an impressive training camp, and he could be a factor Sunday night. On defense, second-year pro David Walker is developing into a dynamic pass rusher who could pressure Williams into mistakes.

5. The Bucs defense has been playing excellent football. What is the key to this unit that sets them apart?

Bowles overhauled his defense in the offseason, adding veterans like Al-Quadin Muhammad, A'Shawn Robinson, Alex Anzalone, and rookie Josiah Trotter. It took a few weeks, but this revamped unit has grown comfortable with the aggressive, blitzing schemes Bowles is known for. Cornerbacks Jacob Parrish (league-leading 13 pass breakups) and Zyon McCollum have been exceptional.

#2960. Watch Vermont field hockey's title-clincher in penalty shootout for America East crown

The Vermont field hockey program is NCAA Tournament-bound for the second time in three years.

The fifth-seeded Catamounts (14-5) survived a thrilling penalty shootout at No. 1 Albany (11-7) to claim the America East Conference tournament championship on Friday, Nov. 6. The two teams were knotted at 2-2 through regulation and two overtime periods before Vermont outlasted the Great Danes, 6-5, in the shootout.

In Friday's league championship, Anyia Woods and Jaime Lewis staked Vermont to a 2-0 halftime lead with a pair of second-quarter goals.

Albany stormed back with two of its own goals in the fourth quarter, both via Quinty Reumer, to level the contest at 2.

Still tied through two OT periods, Emily Enden provided the winner in the shootout. The freshman midfielder from Germany made a nifty spin to beat the Albany goalie to send the Catamounts through.

In 2024, the Catamounts won the program's first America East championship. Last year in their title defense, the Cats lost in overtime to New Hampshire in the final.

Vermont will learn its opponent and site for the 2026 NCAA Tournament on Sunday, Nov. 8. The selection show will stream at NCAA.com starting at 10 p.m.

#2959. $6.3 billion Binance hack is largest crypto theft on record

Binance said Friday that a security incident impacted about $6.3 billion in client assets, with around $3 billion frozen on-chain, marking the largest crypto hack on record.

The company later added it began recovering assets impacted by the breach after pledging to cover losses using its Secure Asset Fund for Users, which the company created in 2018 as a protection for clients in the event of a hack.

It wasn't clear how the attack took place. The world's largest crypto exchange initially said the hack may involve "a compromised DevOps engineer's laptop, which allowed an attacker to gain access to a service container and submit unauthorized withdrawal transactions." However, Co-CEO Richard Teng later said the attack involved a more complex process rather than the misuse of a single engineer's permissions.

The FBI said Friday it was aware of the incident and was in touch with "relevant public and private partners to understand the scope of the activity, evaluate ​potential impacts, ​and ⁠coordinate appropriate investigative steps."

It wasn't clear either who was responsible for the breach. But TRM Labs, a blockchain intelligence and AI investigations company, pointed to North Korean hackers as the potential culprits. "Several features of this attack match earlier North Korea-linked heists, including the Bybit hack and the KelpDAO hack in April 2026: small Tornado Cash withdrawals funding an operator wallet, staging that began well before the theft, and signatures obtained in advance then executed in one burst," the company said in a post.

Blockchain data platform Chainalysis also said this appears "be the work of North Korean cybercrime group TraderTraitor. This group has repeatedly targeted the cryptocurrency industry, exchanges, DeFi protocols, and investors, to raise funds for the regime. We base our intelligence assessment on on-chain activity observed in the hours following the hack."

Binance temporarily suspended withdrawals while it overhauls its withdrawal security systems and has revoked the affected address authorizations, Teng said. The exchange is also working with law enforcement and blockchain intelligence firms to trace and recover the stolen funds, while deposits remain unaffected.

Binance co-founder Yi He said deposits remained unaffected and continued to operate normally, while the exchange was working on enhancements to its withdrawal service and related address controls. It expects to begin resuming withdrawal services within the next 48 hours.

"We have found no evidence of any wallet private key leak. We will share a detailed technical post-mortem once the investigation is complete and the facts are fully verified," Binance said.

The announcement followed moves by several crypto firms, including Circle Internet Group, Tether and Ripple, to freeze funds tied to the hack.

Exploits are not uncommon in the crypto space. In 2025, Bybit suffered roughly $1.5 billion in losses due to a hack. More recently, Bitget lost more than $350 million worth of digital assets in a cyber theft incident. Over the past several years, bad actors have also drained billions of dollars in crypto from decentralized exchanges.

This week's breach "proves our exact point in proposing regulations for crypto markets earlier this month," Michael Selig, chairman of the Commodity Futures Trading Commission, said Friday in a CNBC "Squawk on the Street" interview. The U.S. derivatives regulator on Oct. 5 proposed a framework that would effectively put crypto exchanges under federal government oversight — after Congress failed to approve legislation to regulate the asset.

"The real issue here is that these are unregulated offshore exchanges, and they don't have the same sorts of safeguards that we have in our financial markets here in the United States. We want to offer a federal option to these exchanges to come and register with the CFTC, and they would be subject to the same sorts of cyber security safeguards that we have in our markets today," he said.

Bitcoin fell more than 4% to $77,187.60 on Friday as the hack weighed on crypto markets.

Circle CEO Jeremy Allaire said Thursday evening in a social media post that the company had frozen more than 800 million in USD Coin associated with the incident. USDT stablecoin issuer Tether CEO Paolo Ardoino also said in on X that his firm had frozen more than 1.5 billion of its stablecoin that may have been related to a potential breach.

Jack McDonald, Ripple's senior vice president of stablecoins, said it was actively freezing affected funds held in RLUSD, its U.S. dollar-backed stablecoin.

#2956. Battle of the Bay: Raider Nation versus Niner Gang

The San Francisco 49ers host the Las Vegas Raiders on Sunday at Levi's Stadium. The focus on this used to be bragging rights for Raider Nation vs. Niner Gang as both teams were Bay Area rivals until the Raiders moved to Las Vegas.

In the grand scheme of things, it's just another game on the schedule for both teams. However, it's a bigger game for the Raiders than the 49ers, as the Raiders need a statement win against a top team. Bringing a (4-4) record back to the Bay is cool and all, but for a fringe team on the outside looking in, it's a measuring stick. Head coach Klint Kubiak is no stranger to playing and defeating the 49ers, but he is also a member of the Kyle Shanahan coaching tree.

The strength of the Raiders is in their offense, specifically their passing game. However, since making the switch to Mike Washington Jr. as running back 1, with former first-round pick Ashton Jeanty as a complementary back, the rushing attack has picked up steam and a season-high 38 points were scored.

Against the 49ers, the Raiders face one of the toughest defenses they have seen all season. As if that weren't enough, it's a defense starting to get its fangs back. While the 49ers defense may not be as great as it once was, it's still plenty good enough to bully the Raiders offense. Fred Warner is still amongst the top dogs at linebacker, Dre Greenlaw is hitting anything not in the same colored jersey before and after the whistle. Nick Bosa is finally feeling healthy and the reacquisition of DeForest Buckner is ready to challenge the Raiders' pass protection and newfound success on the ground.

Shanahan has turned the 49ers into perennial playoff contenders behind both an ever-evolving offense and a ferocious defense. Just when teams think they have the offense pegged, they find a different way to exploit the matchups and personnel and break them down. Kubiak was Shanahan's former passing game coordinator. Now, his little brother, Klay Kubiak, is the offensive coordinator for the 49ers.

Klay benefits from experimenting against a Top-5 NFL defense in practice every day. Sunday, he will be calling plays against one of the most underperforming defenses in the league. Furthermore, the 49ers offense relies on scheming players open, utilizing rhythm, timing and deception. All things that Rob Leonard and the Raiders' defense have struggled with over the last five weeks.

Leonard's seat has to be getting hotter and hotter each week another team drops 30+ on his defense. Yes, this was the murderers' row stretch of opponents. There are no excuses for losing; you are what your record says you are, and the Raiders are a .500 team because the offense is good and the defense isn't. Sure, Leonard has the unenviable task of trying to corral a supremely efficient offensive machine. But they don't call it "The Battle of the Bay" for no reason.

Let's be honest, life has been hard for Raider Nation. All the losing and watching members of the Nation convert to the Bang Bang Niner Gang. Why? Because the 49ers have had their stuff together and just kept winning. Growing up in the Bay Area, this rivalry means a little something extra. It is a little bit more personal than a divisional matchup because most Bay Area families and friends are split down the middle.

Knocking those smug looks off the faces of the Niners Faithful just really warms the hearts of members of Raider Nation. The bragging rights silence endless ridicule and incessant reckless rambling, but not only that; it allows the Nation to hold their heads high no matter how the season ends, provided the 49ers don't win it all.

#2954. Observations after Sixers beat Bulls to reach East-leading 7-1

Observations after Sixers beat Bulls to reach East-leading 7-1 originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

The Sixers kept up their hot start to the season Monday night at Xfinity Mobile Arena, reaching 7-1 with a 126-107 win against the Bulls.

All five of the Sixers' starters scored in double figures. Tyrese Maxey posted 26 points on 10-for-15 shooting and 11 assists. Jaylen Brown tallied a season-best 25 points and 11 rebounds.

VJ Edgecombe's 21 points were a new season high. LeBron James recorded 15 points, nine assists and seven boards.

Bulls rookie Caleb Wilson had 24 points and seven rebounds.

Joel Embiid (15 points, eight rebounds) had been listed as questionable with an official designation of "right foot plantar fascia injury management." He sat out Saturday on the second night of a back-to-back for the Sixers' win over the Hornets.

"Plantar is a little bit of a come and go type of thing," Sixers head coach Nick Nurse told reporters pregame. "They're just trying to manage it. It's very painful, if you've ever had it. It can kind of come and go out of nowhere. So we're just trying to manage the pain with that, be as smart as we can."

Nurse told reporters that Dominick Barlow is set to see a specialist Tuesday about the back injury that's sidelined him to start the season.

"He's been on court a little bit … not a ton of moving around," Nurse said. "I think they want to get to tomorrow's appointment. Hopefully, it'll be positive and we can get into his workouts a little bit."

The Sixers will begin a three-game road trip Wednesday when they visit the Pistons. Here are observations on their victory Monday:

After Embiid blitzed a Bulls pick-and-roll, Maxey picked off Matas Buzelis' hurried pass to get the Sixers rolling in transition and James jammed home the team's first field goal.

Edgecombe got a slam soon after James grabbed a defensive rebound and the Sixers again pushed the pace. The Sixers scored six quick fast-break points, leading the Bulls to burn an early timeout, and James' long-distance feeds were a major asset.

"If you're open, he's going to pass it," Edgecombe said of James. "The main thing is just being ready. Tonight I had to run and go get 'em. If you're open and he sees you, he's going to pass the ball."

Edgecombe had a dunk-heavy first quarter. He threw down his second when Dean Wade nabbed a steal and dropped off a typically selfless dish to the Sixers' explosive second-year guard, whose slam put the team up 22-13.

The Sixers have an abundance of dangerous options in the open floor, Edgecombe included. As he's already shown with his continued clutch shotmaking, the 21-year-old won't become anything resembling a non-factor offensively this year as a byproduct of the Sixers' enhanced star power.

Over the team's first seven games, Edgecombe had averaged 11.3 points. He scored 11 in the first period on 5-for-5 shooting.

Adem Bona missed a dunk early in the second quarter, which stung a bit more when Jalen Smith drilled a three-pointer seconds later.

Bona remained the Sixers' main backup center. He had a poor first-half stint overall amid a largely negative start to his third season. The 23-year-old big man played well in the Sixers' victory over the Bucks, posting six points, 13 rebounds and three blocks. Outside of that, his season has been sparse on bright moments so far. It's reasonable to wonder when Nurse might turn to alternatives like Jabari Walker, who played a good game Saturday in Charlotte, or try James again at the five.

The Bulls outscored the Sixers by eight points in Bona's first run, cutting their derficit to 40-38. Chicago went in front with a three by former Sixer Buddy Hield.

The Sixers regained the advantage late in the second quarter. Maxey and James each were excellent as passers in the first half, combining for 14 assists and three turnovers. James fired the ball to Wade for a corner three that gave the Sixers a 60-52 lead.

Following a third-quarter Embiid fadeaway jumper and two James lay-ins, the Sixers were up 17 points.

The Bulls cut the Sixers' lead down to 11 points by the start of the fourth quarter. With Bona still struggling and on four fouls, Wade began the fourth at center. Bona then briefly spelled Wade before being whistled for his fifth foul and taking a seat. Embiid ultimately wasn't needed for any of the fourth period and only logged 25 minutes.

Embiid said postgame that he was not available to return to the action in the fourth and has been feeling his plantar fasciitis on the court. The seven-time All-Star's health is clearly not close to 100 percent.

"I was on a minutes restriction," he said. "(The foot) has been going like that for the past few weeks. It's painful. Just got to manage it.

"If it flares up pretty bad, got to take a step back. We've been able to manage it, but it has its ups and downs."

The Sixers' lead got as low as eight points, but Chicago did not shoot well enough from three-point range (26.3 percent) or have sufficient firepower to sustain a meaningful comeback run. The bottom line is the Sixers are in a different class than the Bulls, who fell to 1-6.

As for the Sixers, they'll wake up Tuesday morning at the top of the Eastern Conference standings.

"You've got some of these nights where you kind of call them professional wins. …You look at the records and all that kind of stuff, but they have a lot of talent," Nurse said. "A lot of these teams play a way that's a little scary. It's fast and it's furious, lots of threes. … They're going to keep coming at you, high-possession games. So they're not easy games and I'm glad that we handled it professionally tonight."

#2953. Mike Vrabel focused on supporting TreVeyon Henderson amid medical issue

New England Patriots running back TreVeyon Henderson revealed Friday that he has been dealing with a neurological medical condition that resulted in hospitalization.

Henderson was first listed on the injury report with an illness last Thursday, Oct. 29, and has been away from the team since.

#2950. ‘I'm finding my footing': Jaylen Brown's role taking shape with Sixers

'I'm finding my footing': Jaylen Brown's role taking shape with Sixers originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

Jaylen Brown has spent the opening weeks of the season describing the Sixers as a work in progress.

He offered a similarly humble perspective Monday night at Xfinity Mobile Arena. His performance, however, provided the clearest look yet at how he can influence this group while sharing the floor with the club's other established stars.

Brown finished with 25 points, 11 rebounds and five assists in the Sixers' 126-107 win over the Chicago Bulls. He shot 9 for 15 from the field, made four three-pointers and did not commit a turnover.

Five of his rebounds came on the offensive end, an area Brown has emphasized while the Sixers continue piecing together their frontcourt rotation.

"Just a mindset," Brown said. "Just trying to create extra opportunities for my team. As we're trying to patch together our fives and our front line, I try to make an extra contribution and emphasis on the glass."

Brown has also been careful about how he describes his place on a roster that already belonged to Joel Embiid and Tyrese Maxey.

Maxey scored 15 of his 26 points in the fourth quarter Monday, continuing his torrid start to the season. He has led the Sixers in scoring while being the only starter to appear in all eight games.

Brown is not interested in interrupting that.

"Maxey's been leading the way, 100 percent," Brown said. "He's been here. This is his team. Him and Joel have been here for a while, so the mindset has been to complement those guys. I'm finding my footing, but Maxey has been leading the way for sure."

Brown's footing looked firm against Chicago.

Beyond his scoring, he helped the Sixers control the glass, moved the ball within an offense that generated 23 assists during the first half and supplied the physicality Nick Nurse expects from him.

"The dude is tough and he wins," Nurse said. "I'll take that all day long. The degree of difficulty on some of the shots he hit was really amazing. I thought he pounded the glass and got some key rebounds. Makes a lot of winning plays. Man, he's a winner."

Brown spent the first 10 years of his career in Boston. He won a championship there, reached another NBA Finals and grew from a 20-year-old prospect into one of the league's premier two-way wings.

Moving into a new system alongside James, Embiid and Maxey was never going to be seamless.

"I mean, he's fitting in along with all of us," James said. "We're getting better and better. It's not going to happen overnight. He spent his first 10 years in a system in Boston, in the city, so it's going to take some adjustment.

"It takes adjustment for him. It's going to take adjustment for the rest of the guys that joined this franchise as well. But tonight was a great step."

James pointed to Brown's "pressure, his shot-making ability, his rebounding ability" and his professionalism.

Brown's early influence has extended beyond his production.

The Sixers entered Monday with one of the NBA's best half-court defenses. Brown has been one of the group's most persistent voices, stressing communication, preparation and attention to opposing players' tendencies.

"That's the part that I care about the most as a group, not individually," Brown said. "Having a mentality night in and night out, respecting your opponent, respecting the game, making sure that we are executing our coverages, we're being clear in our communication and then we're also taking away tendencies on a night-to-night basis.

"Those things I just explained are over 50 percent of the battle."

Brown understands that maintaining those details across an 82-game schedule will require more than reminders.

"A little bit of accountability," he said. "You learn different guys require different things. How you communicate with each guy may be a little different, but accountability for sure and confrontation if need be.

"The offense is going to be what it's going to be, but our defense has got to be non-negotiable. That's something I say all the time. Non-negotiable. You want to be a good team, defense is non-negotiable."

The Sixers showed both sides of their defensive potential Monday.

They built a double-digit lead during the first half, allowed Chicago to score 36 points in the second quarter and then created separation after halftime. Philadelphia held the Bulls to 20 points in the third quarter before pulling away in the fourth.

Brown did not celebrate their ability to raise their intensity after it slipped.

"I don't like that we have that," he said. "It's good that we have shown the potential, but we've got to keep that switch on. It doesn't work like that where you just flip the switch.

"It can catch up to us and it can bite us against a better team or on a night that we're not being as crisp on offense. It can't be a switch just because we're playing a lesser opponent or whatever the case may be. We've got to make sure that mentality is from the beginning to end. Can't skip any steps."

That urgency accompanies considerable optimism.

Brown said the Sixers enjoy playing together. He loves when James grabs a rebound because "he's like a quarterback," capable of launching an outlet pass or immediately pushing the pace.

The group is learning when to attack, when to move the ball and how to avoid hesitating in its effort to play unselfishly.

"It's a lot of talent, man," Brown said. "We like each other, and we're just kind of building that chemistry of how we can get this thing to a well-oiled machine. I think we're taking steps forward every single day."

The Sixers are 7-1 despite Brown believing those steps remain incomplete. They have won close games, erased sizable deficits and received important contributions from different players.

Brown's performance Monday showed another version of how their pieces can fit.

"We still have a long way to go to being a really, really solid, great team," Brown said. "But we've been able to win some games. We started out the season great, 7-1, and you haven't seen our best version yet.

#2949. Why Max Verstappen ended F1 practice early in Brazil – Red Bull explains

Max Verstappen finished Friday's Formula 1 practice sessions in Sao Paulo in third place. The Dutchman occupied that position in both sessions, in fact, and was also around two and a half tenths off the fastest driver in each session, with Lando Norris topping the first one and Kimi Antonelli the second.

Red Bull concludes that there is once again "enough potential" in the car to fight at the front, although not everything went smoothly for Verstappen at Interlagos. During the first practice session of the day, he complained – as usual – about the downshifts, while a problem with the power steering gave him a "proper workout" towards the end of the session.

"Unacceptable downshifts" – Max Verstappen explains what's behind a recurring F1 radio complaint

In second practice, Verstappen provided one of the most entertaining team radio exchanges of the F1 season so far when he did not immediately understand a message from Tom Hart – who is once again replacing Gianpiero Lambiase as his race engineer this weekend – about the power unit and gearbox.

"So we have nothing on the switch, but they are adapting to you", Hart said over the team radio. It prompted Verstappen to respond in an almost sing-song voice: "What does that mean? I have to speak to the engine myself? I think I should be like 'oh, clutch, I'm switching! You'll be punished otherwise!'"

More noteworthy was the fact that Verstappen ended the second practice session early. With just over six minutes remaining on the clock, the four-time world champion was already heading into the pitlane, apparently at his own suggestion, judging by the team radio exchanges.

Verstappen suggested coming in, to which Hart replied: "Okay, Max. If you want to box, then come in at the end of this lap. Radio has gone poor, for info."

At the end of the practice day in Sao Paulo, Red Bull's head of race strategy Hannah Schmitz explained why Verstappen had returned to the pits early: "So it was just [because of] finding a lot of inconsistencies in the long run and we didn't feel like it was helpful anymore.

"We weren't getting the data we needed. We knew the things that we wanted to be looking at and changing overnight, so we just went straight into that."

That is particularly noteworthy, as coming in early also meant Verstappen gave up the opportunity to carry out a practice start on the grid. Given that the grid at the Autodromo José Carlos Pace slopes quite steeply uphill and Red Bull's starts have not always been particularly good this year, that is remarkable.

After Verstappen got out of the car, mechanics were seen working underneath his RB22.

As for the competitive picture, Schmitz concludes that there should once again be plenty of potential for this weekend, after Verstappen won three of the last four Grands Prix.

"I think everyone agrees that there's a lot of potential within the car, but with both drivers there are things that they want to look into and change overnight. But in a way that's a good thing because that means we know what's happening on the car and what we can address.

"There's a lot of potential and optimism for the rest of the weekend."

However, the weather forecast also needs to be taken into account. With Schmitz's run plan, Red Bull tried to get a good understanding of all three tyre compounds, although the question remains how valuable that data will be given the changing temperatures and the possibility of rain.

"I think it was a very close call on which sessions you ran the hard and which session you ran the medium today", Schmitz said.

"I think all of the top teams kind of tested all three compounds because they are so similar. It comes down a lot to driver preference, team tendency and preference, and those are the kind of things we're trying to get to the bottom of.

"Today, obviously the conditions are quite different to what's forecast for Sunday, so now we have to look at how we think that's going to translate to what happens on Sunday. And I think that's the difficult thing."

Max Verstappen jokes about rain threat in Brazil: "I've installed antivirus now!"

#2947. The midterm election could reshape the muni bond market. Here’s what investors need to know

The outcome of Tuesday's election is expected to have broad implications across the market, including municipal bonds.

The spotlight has been on the battle for congressional control and whether Democrats will wrest the House of Representatives, the Senate or both from Republicans. But for the muni market, what happens at the state level could have bigger repercussions — at least in the near term.

There is an array of initiatives on ballots around the nation that could change tax laws and add to bond supply. Taxes matter to muni investors since the income received is free of federal tax, as well as state tax if the holder lives in the state where the bond is issued.

Depending on the location, ballot measures are up for approval that would either provide tax breaks, or raise taxes on the wealthy.

"It's a complete bifurcation between red and blue states," said Dan Close, head of municipals at Nuveen. "Blue states are trying to put in place graduated income tax structures, trying to capture more revenue from high-income earners. Red states are promoting affordability by trying to cut property taxes."

Red states are those that predominantly vote Republican, while blue states swing toward Democrats.

For instance, California has a proposition known as the "billionaire tax," which would impose a one-time levy equal to 5% of the wealth of certain taxpayers with at least $1 billion in assets. The state also has a measure on the ballot that would make a temporary tax hike permanent on individuals earning over $371,000, adjusted annually for inflation. The tax is currently set to expire in 2031.

"The impact to the muni bond market will be to make the exemption more valuable," Close said of the possible passage of tax increases. Investors will likely seek shelter in the muni market, which would send bond prices higher, he noted.

It is a different story in Florida, where voters are set to decide whether to increase the Sunshine State's homestead exemption for non-school district property taxes. Qualified homeowners currently have a $25,000 property tax exemption, as well as another for $25,000 that doesn't apply to school property taxes. The new proposal raises the latter exemption to $150,000 in 2027 and $250,000 by 2028. New residents have a five-year waiting period before receiving the benefit.

If passed, some local governments will have to find other revenue sources or increase the overall tax rate to replace what they've lost, said Tom Kozlik, head of public policy and municipal strategy at HilltopSecurities.

"It's not necessarily going to result in downgrades for sure, but there would have to be some balancing with regard to overall revenues," he said.

Not all municipalities will be hurt equally, which means credit research is important, said Close.

"Like everything in municipal finance, it is really on a case-by-case basis that you could have some credit deterioration from [a] rollback of property taxes," he said.

Other voters on Tuesday will be asked to approve more state or local government debt. That could weigh on a market that has already seen heightened supply.

Some $473.8 billion has already been issued year to date, through September, according to the Securities Industry and Financial Markets Association. That's a 7.8% increase over the same period a year ago. In 2025, total muni issuance reached $587.3 billion, 14.3% higher than 2024, Sifma said.

Cooper Howard, director of fixed income research and strategy at the Schwab Center for Financial Research, believes supply is the biggest issue coming out of the election.

"That amount of issuance has been one of the reasons why you have to see that strong amount of demand continue to keep pace with it for total returns to keep up," Howard said.

Fortunately, new supply doesn't necessarily come quickly into the market. "There's a window over a couple years that they can issue," he noted.

The ultimate makeup of the next Congress could also have consequences for muni investors as lawmakers sort out their priorities. Most notable is what lawmakers will do to tackle the country's affordability problem, Kozlik at Hilltop said.

"There could be some tax policy initiatives and even affordability-related initiatives that manifest themselves in the first quarter of next year," he said. "That could impact housing. It could impact higher education. It could impact public power financing."

Investors considering moves before the election may want to look at those states with tax increases on the ballot, said Close at Nuveen.

For instance, Nuveen recently began offering a separately managed account dedicated to Washington after getting an enormous number of inquiries from financial advisors. The state recently enacted a 9.9% tax on individuals with taxable annual income above $1 million, set to go into effect in 2028. But the issue is now being put to voters, who can decide whether to repeal the new law.

"At the margin, if you do think that some of these measures are going to pass, knowing that bonds usually move up with an increase in taxes, there might be some opportunities to buy ahead of the election for blue states," Close said.

Howard believes munis are attractive right now, particularly for those who are in a tax bracket of 24% or above. Fundamentals are good and the bonds largely have good credit quality, he said. "About 70% of the broad muni index is either triple-A or double-A rated."

Kozlik is also bullish, calling yields "really attractive." He suggests investors concentrate on AA-rated or higher bonds.

"The muni space is part of the portfolio that most investors want," he said. "It helps them sleep well at night."

#2946. Observations after Sixers earn strong road win over Pistons, Brown scores 30

Observations after Sixers earn strong road win over Pistons, Brown scores 30 originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

The Sixers remain the Eastern Conference's sole leader.

They earned a 112-106 road win over the Pistons on Wednesday night to move to 8-1 on the season and stay at the top of the standings. Detroit fell to 6-2.

The Sixers last started 8-1 in the 2023-24 season, which was Nick Nurse's first as the team's head coach.

Jaylen Brown scored a season-high 30 points and pulled down seven rebounds.

Joel Embiid had 26 points and a season-best 15 rebounds. LeBron James posted 13 points, 10 rebounds and eight assists. Tyrese Maxey added 19 points and seven assists.

The Pistons' leading scorers were Duncan Robinson with 27 points and Cade Cunningham with 26.

The Sixers were down Dominick Barlow (lower back stress reaction) and Ariel Hukporti (right Achilles tendon tear). The Pistons' injured players included Jalen Duren (right hamstring strain) and Isaiah Joe (right hip contusion).

Nurse told reporters in Detroit he thinks "it'll be another 10 to 12 days before they kind of clear (Barlow) for getting on the court to see where he is," according to Sixers Wire's Ky Carlin.

The Sixers will visit the Cavaliers on Friday night. Here are observations on their victory over Detroit:

Embiid assisted the Sixers' first basket with a sharp pass out of a double team. He saw Brown's cut along the baseline and dished to him for a layup.

Detroit ran several double drag screen actions early, forcing Embiid to defend on the perimeter. The Pistons started 7 for 11 from the floor and took a 17-11 edge on a Robinson three-pointer.

The Sixers then made a 10-0 run and Embiid was right in the middle of it. He converted a put-back layup after a pretty high-low feed from James. Embiid hit two foul shots to give the Sixers the lead. The team raised its defensive level and Detroit's offense grew frigid.

Embiid was very good on the glass in the first half, grabbing eight rebounds. The Sixers had an excellent rebounding game as a team, limiting the Pistons to four offensive boards over 48 minutes.

Embiid didn't break out of his early-season three-point shooting slump, going 0 for 3. He's 3 for 26 (11.5 percent) on the season and hasn't made a three since the Sixers' second game.

Late in the second quarter, Embiid got into a dead-ball dust-up with Tolu Smith. After an officials' review, Embiid was assessed a technical foul for "initiating" the altercation.

Playing on his 26th birthday, Maxey missed his first four field goals and was scoreless until he hit a floater with 31.5 seconds left in the first quarter.

Brown picked up the scoring slack, tallying 10 points in the first on 4-for-6 shooting and hitting a deep, buzzer-beating three to put the Sixers up 29-21. He produced at all three levels, chose smart spots to attack and looked comfortable in the rhythm of the Sixers' offense.

Meanwhile, James did his usuall stellar work as a passer. He assisted two Adem Bona slams in the second quarter and constantly spotted open teammates. Nurse rewarded Bona's strong opening stint, playing him for a lengthy stretch.

Brown swished a corner triple to extend the Sixers' lead to 48-31. With a 3-for-5 night, Brown is at 45.7 percent from long distance so far this season. It wouldn't be surprising at all if Brown's three-point shooting improved from last season's 34.7 percent clip. With such an abundance of star power, the Sixers' hope will be that everyone gets regular open looks beyond the arc throughout the year.

As for Maxey, he sat with 3:26 left in the second quarter after being whistled for his third foul. Even with a mere two points from Maxey, the Sixers entered halftime holding a 14-point advantage.

The Pistons played better basketball to begin the third quarter.

Following a John Collins alley-oop jam, Nurse called timeout with his team's lead down to single digits. The Pistons' offense sustained some success, but the Sixers prevented a sustained run.

Embiid played an especially physical, aggressive third quarter. He tipped in his own miss and Maxey then sunk a three to make the Sixers' lead 84-68. They were up as many as 17 points in the third.

Embiid sat out the fourth quarter Monday night for the Sixers' win over the Bulls and said he was on a minutes restriction (25) because of his right foot plantar fasciitis. He played the fourth period against the Pistons and logged 33 minutes. Bona went back to the locker room early in the fourth. Nurse told reporters postgame that Bona had "neck soreness."

Detroit did not let the Sixers coast to victory.

Robinson drained a three. Cunningham made a pair of free throws and drove in for a layup that cut the Pistons' deficit to 106-102.

The Sixers halted the Pistons' run before anything disastrous was on the table. Brown blocked a Cunningham three-point try, Embiid knocked down two foul shots, and the East's No. 1 early-season team improved to 4-0 on the road.

#2944. Cleveland Browns vs. New Orleans Saints: Week 9 Need to Know

The Cleveland Browns close out their three-game road trip on Sunday when they face the New Orleans Saints.

What would have been an otherwise humdrum game has taken on a new level of something between excitement and dread with the news that the Browns will start Shedeur Sanders at quarterback.

Sanders will get his first start of the season thanks to Deshaun Watson still being in the league-mandated concussion protocol. And not to put too much pressure on Sanders, but the Browns are in the heat of a playoff race while the Saints are simply trying to get out of their own way each week as they bumble through the season.

It's the Browns vs. the Saints from New Orleans, and here is everything to know about the game.

Records: Cleveland is 5-3. New Orleans is 2-5.

Stadium: Caesars Superdome, New Orleans, La.

Announcers: Andrew Catalon, Logan Ryan, and A.J. Ross (sideline reporter)

Radio: 850 ESPN Cleveland, 92.3 The Fan WKRK, 98.5 WNCX, 1300 AM La Mega (Español)

Announcers: Andrew Siciliano, Nathan Zegura, and Je'Rod Cherry (sidelines)

Last meeting: The Saints won the last meeting, 35-14, in Week 11 of the 2024 season.

First meeting: The Browns won the first meeting, 42-7, on October 1, 1967.

All-time series: Cleveland leads the all-time series, 13-7. Eight of those 13 wins by the Browns have come in New Orleans.

Weather: The game will be played indoors, so weather is not a concern.

Uniform: The Browns will wear their white jersey with their horrible brown pants.

Injury report: Browns – Out: Defensive tackle Maliek Collins (elbow). Questionable: Quarterback Deshaun Watson (concussion). Saints – Out: Running back CJ Donaldson (shoulder, concussion). Questionable: Offensive tackle Kelvin Banks Jr. (ankle), cornerback Martin Emerson Jr. (shin), offensive tackle Asim Richards (thumb), and wide receiver Jordyn Tyson (hamstring

The line: Browns +3 points, with an over/under of 42.5 points, per FanDuel.

The Saints rank No. 28 in rushing, averaging 86 yards per game.

Cleveland's defense ranks No. 23 against the run, allowing 126.9 rushing yards per game.

The Browns rank No. 24 in rushing, averaging 97.6 yards per game.

New Orleans' defense ranks No. 29 against the run, allowing 139.4 rushing yards per game.

New Orleans wide receiver Chris Olave has more receptions (49) and receiving yards (636) than the rest of the Saints wide receivers combined, according to ESPN Research.

The Saints have only scored on one offensive drive in the first quarter this season.

Cleveland's defense has allowed a score on the first offensive drive in seven of their eight games.

The Browns lead the NFL in pre-snap penalties (35) and total penalties (85).

Cleveland quarterback Shedeur Sanders made seven starts as a rookie, posting a total QBR of 19, which ranks No. 691 out of 696 quarterbacks with at least 200 pass attempts since 2006, according to ESPN.

During those starts, Sanders averaged 3.24 seconds to throw, the slowest among 38 quarterbacks who made at least six starts in 2025.

The Browns have gone 83 consecutive drives without scoring a touchdown on a series where the quarterback has been sacked, according to The Athletic.

Cleveland offensive coordinator Todd Monken on what he saw from quarterback Shedeur Sanders this week in practice:

"I liked his confidence, like the way he operated; he looked very comfortable in terms of getting our guys in and out of the huddle. Getting to the line of scrimmage was going to be half the battle this week, obviously, going there. I thought he threw the ball, you know, extremely well.

"I do think there are positives to the amount of reps he got during the fall. I think obviously there was a two-month gap there, but I think there was enough there in terms of our system, how we do things. He's been on every trip; he's been on the headset during games. He hears every call that we make. So, if a player is diligent like he's been, then you expect there to be some carryover."

#2943. LeBron James is having fun in Philly and one fan's wine can prove it

LeBron James is having fun in Philly and one fan's wine can prove it originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

LeBron James is one of kind in so many ways. What he did Monday night might be the cherry on top.

In the third quarter of the Sixers' 126-107 victory over the Bulls, James decided he'd take fan interaction to a whole new level.

James looked for a transition pass from Tyrese Maxey, who snagged a rebound. James was behind the pass and his momentum took him into the stands, where he disappeared for a few moments.

It wasn't until another angle was shown on replay that we could see what actually went down in the darkness of the seats. James didn't hold back giving reporters a play-by-play.

"I spilled some of his wine," he said postgame. "I crashed into him, spilled some of his wine. So I wanted to see if it was worth it on my arm and how good it was. It was alright."

Troy Clemons – the Sixers fan whose drink spilled – explained his perspective on the interaction with James to Kris Rhim of ESPN.

"[I was] walking to my seat … LeBron crashed into me, he grabbed me, he said 'My bad,' my drink spilled everywhere. He took my drink from me and he basically just took a sip of it, gave it back and ran back on the [court]."

"At the end of the day he's another man like me," Clemons said. "I mean he's LeBron, but at the end of the day, I threw that cup in the trash. And it's nothing up with him, I just got a fresh drink though."

It should be noted that players are not supposed to drink from fans' cups, but it's rare for the NBA to fine someone for a spontaneous interaction such as this. In 2019, a similar thing happened when Mike Scott tumbled into the Bucks' court-side seats. The Sixers star jokingly took a sip of a fan's beverage and the NBA opted not to fine him. Time will tell if James has the same fate.

If nothing else, an interaction like this just shows how much fun James seems to be having.

The 24-year veteran noted the importance of finding joy in the game as he grinds through the season.

"It's a long marathon. And if you don't find ways to have fun throughout it, even though you're working and even though you're locked in, then it could wear on you. So for me, especially at this point in my career, being able to just appreciate the moments, the small moments, these fans are great to us. We try to be great to them as well. By playing the game the right way, playing the game hard and then also just being showmen as well, it was a great game for us."

Head coach Nick Nurse also weighed in on the 41-year-old's mindset coming into the season.

"This summer I think he probably stated publicly that he wanted to go some place and have some fun playing. He loves the game, [it's his] 24th year. It's a group of guys, like I keep saying, that have been super focused in the intent of playing the right way. I think he's a big part of that."

The vibes are high. It's difficult for them not to be.

With Monday's win against the Bulls, the Sixers are 7-1 and lead the Eastern Conference. They've won four in a row and the only team they've lost to is the Pistons. The Sixers will face them on Wednesday to start a three-game road trip.
